\mathbf{p}\cdot\partial_{t}\mathbf{p}=0
:

\displaystyle\lambda_{p}=-K\left(\partial_{x}\theta\right)^{2}-\gamma\omega_{n}(\nu\sin 2\theta-\nu_{\rm PC}\cos 2\theta)~.

(368)

Using Eq.
368
allows to evaluate the molecular field
\mathbf{h}
, Eqs.
364
-
365
, and the tension component
t_{xy}
, Eq.
K.1
.
Imposing
t_{xy}=0
one then obtains for
\omega_{n}
:

\displaystyle\omega_{n}=

\displaystyle\frac{\partial_{x}v_{y}}{2}=\frac{1}{(4\eta+\gamma(\nu\sin 2\theta-\nu_{\rm{PC}}\cos 2\theta)^{2})}\times

\displaystyle\bigg(K\partial_{x}^{2}\theta(1-\nu\cos 2\theta-\nu_{\rm PC}\sin 2\theta)

\displaystyle-\zeta_{\rm n}\Delta\mu\sin 2\theta+\zeta_{{\rm PCn}}\Delta\mu\cos 2\theta\bigg)~,

(369)

which can be plugged in Eq.
367
to obtain a dynamic equation for
\theta
, Eq.
VII.1
.

In the following we consider situations for which
\nu
and
\nu_{\rm PC}
can be neglected,
\nu=\nu_{\rm PC}=0
. In that case the homogeneous steady-state solutions for the angle
\theta=\theta_{s}
satisfy:

\displaystyle\sin(2\theta-\theta_{p})=-\frac{4\eta\lambda_{\rm PC}\cos\theta_{p}}{\zeta_{\rm n}}~,

(370)

where
\theta_{p}
is defined in Eq.
139
. The corresponding steady-state solutions are given in Eq.
143
.

To study the stability of homogeneous steady-states, we now linearise Eq.
VII.1
around the steady-state solutions,
\theta=\theta_{s}+\delta\theta
, and obtain:

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\delta\theta=K\left(\frac{1}{\gamma}+\frac{1}{4\eta}\right)\partial_{x}^{2}\delta\theta-\frac{\zeta_{\rm n}\Delta\mu}{2\eta\cos\theta_{p}}\cos(2\theta_{s}-\theta_{p})\delta\theta~.

(371)

We note that the steady-state solutions
\theta_{s}^{1}
and
\theta_{s}^{2}
satisfy

\displaystyle\cos(2\theta_{s}^{1}-\theta_{p})=-\sqrt{1-\frac{(4\eta\lambda_{\rm PC})^{2}}{\zeta_{{\rm PCn}}^{2}+\zeta_{\rm n}^{2}}}

(372)

\displaystyle\cos(2\theta_{s}^{2}-\theta_{p})=\sqrt{1-\frac{(4\eta\lambda_{\rm PC})^{2}}{\zeta_{{\rm PCn}}^{2}+\zeta_{\rm n}^{2}}}.

(373)

such that when these solutions exist,
\cos(2\theta_{s}^{1}-\theta_{p})\leq 0
and
\cos(2\theta_{s}^{2}-\theta_{p})\geq 0
. Considering Eq.
371
, this implies that the stability of solutions for
L\rightarrow\infty
depends on
\zeta_{\rm n}
:
\theta_{s}^{1}
is stable for
\zeta_{\rm n}<0
,
\theta_{s}^{2}
is stable for
\zeta_{\rm n}>0
.

In the regime where
\theta
is spatially homogeneous and rotates (
L\rightarrow\infty
and
|\frac{\zeta_{\rm n}}{4\eta\lambda_{\rm{PC}}\cos\theta_{p}}|<1
, corresponding to the opposite regime to Eq.
142
), integration of Eq.
VII.1
shows that the period of rotation is given by

\displaystyle T=

\displaystyle\left|\int_{0}^{2\pi}\frac{d\theta}{\lambda_{\rm PC}+\frac{\zeta_{\rm n}\sin(2\theta-\theta_{p})}{4\eta\cos\theta_{p}}}\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{2\pi}{|\lambda_{\rm PC}|\sqrt{1-(\frac{\zeta_{\rm n}}{4\eta\lambda_{\rm PC}\cos\theta_{p}})^{2}}}~.

(374)

In the regime where the polarity is fixed with planar anchoring at the confinement boundaries (
\theta(0)=\theta(L)=\frac{\pi}{2}
), and in the limit
\zeta_{\rm n}=\zeta_{\rm PCn}=\nu=\nu_{\rm PC}=0
, the steady-state profile of
\theta
is:

\displaystyle\theta(x)=\frac{\pi}{2}+\frac{2\eta\gamma\lambda_{\rm PC}\Delta\mu}{K(\gamma+4\eta)}x(x-L)~.

(375)

The number of full spatial rotations of the polarity can be estimated from
n\simeq 2|\theta(x=L/2)-\theta(x=0)|/(2\pi)
, giving here
n\simeq\frac{\eta\gamma|\lambda_{\rm PC}|\Delta\mu L^{2}}{2\pi K(\gamma+4\eta)}
.

K.2
Weakly deformed active film with broken up-down symmetry

Here we consider a weakly deformed active film whose boundaries are enforced to be straight (
\partial_{y}v_{x}(x=0)=\partial_{y}v_{x}(x=L)=0
and
\partial_{y}v_{n}(x=0)=\partial_{y}v_{n}(x=L)=0
) but whose boundaries position can still freely adjust, without external force
t_{xx}(x=0)=t_{xx}(x=L)=t_{xy}(x=0)=t_{xy}(x=L)=0
or bending moment (
\bar{m}_{xx}(x=0)=\bar{m}_{xx}(x=L)=0
). The tangential and normal force balance equations
356
-
358
then imply
t_{xx}=t_{xy}=\bar{m}_{xx}=0
. The incompressibility condition
v_{k}{}^{k}=0
becomes here
\partial_{x}v_{x}+v_{n}C_{xx}=0
. Since calculations are done to first order in the velocity field
\mathbf{v}
and in the surface height gradient
|\partial_{x}h|
, this condition becomes
\partial_{x}v_{x}\simeq 0
and, without loss of generality since this fixes a condition on rigid translation of the system,
v_{x}=0
. This implies that at this order, the change in system size
dL/dt=v_{x}(x=L)-v_{x}(x=0)
can be neglected.

Using Eqs.
146
-
147
and the molecular field expression Eqs
364
-
365
, we obtain to first order in the curvature tensor and in the flow field
v_{y}
the expression for the components
t_{xy}
and
\bar{m}_{xx}
:

\displaystyle t_{xy}=

\displaystyle 2\eta\omega_{n}

\displaystyle+\frac{\nu-\beta C_{xx}}{2}(-(\lambda_{p}+K(\partial_{x}\theta)^{2})\sin 2\theta+K\cos 2\theta\partial_{x}^{2}\theta)

\displaystyle+\frac{(\zeta_{n}+(\tilde{\zeta}^{\prime}_{n}-\zeta_{c{\rm n}})C_{xx})\Delta\mu}{2}\sin 2\theta-\frac{1}{2}K\partial_{x}^{2}\theta~,

(376)

\displaystyle\bar{m}_{xx}=

\displaystyle(\eta_{c}+\eta_{cb})\partial_{t}C_{xx}+(\kappa+K\cos^{2}\theta)C_{xx}

\displaystyle+\zeta_{c}\Delta\mu+\frac{\zeta_{cn}}{2}\Delta\mu\cos 2\theta

\displaystyle+\beta\left(-(\lambda_{p}+K(\partial_{x}\theta)^{2})\frac{\cos 2\theta}{2}-\frac{K}{2}\sin 2\theta\partial_{x}^{2}\theta\right)~.

(377)

where we have used the notation
p_{x}=\cos\theta
,
p_{y}=\sin\theta
. We have also used that to first order in the velocity field and in the curvature tensor,
D_{t}C_{xx}\simeq\partial_{t}C_{xx}
.
Using the polarity dynamic equation, Eq.
148
, we obtain:

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\theta=

\displaystyle\frac{K}{\gamma}\partial_{x}^{2}\theta+\omega_{n}(1-\nu\cos 2\theta)-\frac{1}{2}\lambda_{\rm UD}\Delta\mu\sin 2\theta C_{xx}

\displaystyle+\beta\frac{\sin 2\theta}{4}\partial_{t}C_{xx}~,

(378)

and

\displaystyle\lambda_{p}=

\displaystyle-K\left(\partial_{x}\theta\right)^{2}-\gamma\omega_{n}\nu\sin 2\theta-\frac{1}{2}\gamma\beta\cos^{2}\theta\partial_{t}C_{xx}

\displaystyle+\gamma\lambda_{\rm UD}\Delta\mu C_{xx}\cos^{2}\theta~.

(379)

Setting
t_{xy}=0
in Eq.
376
gives an expression for the gradient of flow
\omega_{n}
, while setting
\bar{m}_{xx}=0
in Eq.
377
gives a dynamic equation for the curvature time derivative,
\partial_{t}C_{xx}
. Combined with Eq.
378
, one obtains two coupled dynamic equations for
\theta
and
C_{xx}
. These equations are lengthy but take a simpler form in the limit
\nu=\beta=0
and
\kappa\gg K
(Eqs.
149
-
150
).

We now discuss the stability of uniform, steady-state solutions of Eqs.
149
-
150
. This analysis is carried out more easily in the limit of fast shape relaxation,
\eta_{c}\rightarrow 0
,
\eta_{cb}\rightarrow 0
. In that case
C_{xx}
relaxes quasi-statically to the steady-state solution of Eq.
149
and the polarity equation becomes:

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\theta=

\displaystyle\frac{K(4\eta+\gamma)}{4\eta\gamma}\partial_{x}^{2}\theta+\sin 2\theta\bar{\lambda}_{\rm UD}\frac{2\bar{\zeta}_{c}+\zeta_{cn}\cos 2\theta}{4\kappa}\Delta\mu^{2}~,

(380)

where for convenience we have introduced the reduced parameters
\bar{\lambda}_{\rm UD}=\lambda_{\rm UD}+(\tilde{\zeta}_{\rm n}^{\prime}-\zeta_{c{\rm n}})/(2\eta)
and
\bar{\zeta}_{c}=\zeta_{c}-\zeta_{\rm n}\kappa/(2\eta\bar{\lambda}_{\rm UD}\Delta\mu)
.

We now linearize around the steady-state solutions,
\theta=\theta_{s}+\delta\theta
, and consider only uniform perturbations. For
\theta_{s}=\pm\frac{\pi}{2}
one obtains:

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\delta\theta=-\frac{\bar{\lambda}_{\rm UD}(2\bar{\zeta}_{c}-\zeta_{c\rm n})(\Delta\mu)^{2}}{2\kappa}\delta\theta~,

(381)

so that the steady-state solutions
\theta_{s}=\pm\frac{\pi}{2}
are stable for
\bar{\lambda}_{\rm UD}(2\bar{\zeta}_{c}-\zeta_{c\rm n})>0
. For
\bar{\lambda}_{\rm UD}>0
, this corresponds to
\zeta_{c\rm n}<2\bar{\zeta_{c}}
. For
\bar{\lambda}_{\rm UD}<0
, this corresponds to
\zeta_{c\rm n}>2\bar{\zeta}_{c}
.

Around
\theta_{s}=0
or
\theta_{s}=\pi
one obtains:

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\delta\theta=\frac{\bar{\lambda}_{\rm UD}(2\bar{\zeta}_{c}+\zeta_{c\rm n})(\Delta\mu)^{2}}{2\kappa}\delta\theta~,

(382)

which is stable for
\bar{\lambda}_{\rm UD}(2\bar{\zeta}_{c}+\zeta_{c\rm n})<0
. For
\bar{\lambda}_{\rm UD}>0
, this corresponds to
\zeta_{c{\rm n}}<-2\bar{\zeta}_{c}
. For
\bar{\lambda}_{\rm UD}<0
, this corresponds to
\zeta_{c\rm n}>-2\bar{\zeta}_{c}
.

Around
\theta_{s}=\theta_{0}
with
\theta_{0}
defined by
\cos 2\theta_{0}=-2\bar{\zeta}_{c}/\zeta_{c\rm n}
(see Eq.
151
; a solution exists only if
|\zeta_{c\rm n}/\bar{\zeta}_{c}|>2
), one obtains:

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\delta\theta=

\displaystyle-\frac{\bar{\lambda}_{\rm UD}\zeta_{c\rm n}\left(1-\frac{4\bar{\zeta}_{c}^{2}}{\zeta_{c\rm n}^{2}}\right)(\Delta\mu)^{2}}{2\kappa}\delta\theta

(383)

Here the corresponding steady-state solutions are stable for
\bar{\lambda}_{\rm UD}\zeta_{c\rm n}\left(\left(\frac{\zeta_{c\rm n}}{\bar{\zeta}_{c}}\right)^{2}-4\right)>0
.

For an inhomogeneous
\theta
perturbation around
\theta_{s}=\frac{\pi}{2}
, one has:

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\delta\theta=\frac{K(4\eta+\gamma)}{4\eta\gamma}\partial_{x}^{2}\delta\theta-\frac{\bar{\lambda}_{\rm UD}(2\bar{\zeta}_{c}-\zeta_{c\rm n})(\Delta\mu)^{2}}{2\kappa}\delta\theta~.

(384)

Considering a confined system with planar anchoring boundary conditions
\theta(0)=\theta(L)=\frac{\pi}{2}
, one has
\delta\theta(0)=\delta\theta(L)=0
. The most unstable mode
\delta\theta=A\sin(\pi\frac{x}{L})
then has an amplitude
A
with the dynamics:

\displaystyle\partial_{t}A=-\left[\frac{K(4\eta+\gamma)\pi^{2}}{4\gamma\eta L^{2}}+\frac{\bar{\lambda}_{\rm UD}(2\bar{\zeta}_{c}-\zeta_{c\rm n})(\Delta\mu)^{2}}{2\kappa}\right]A~.

(385)

If
\bar{\lambda}_{\rm UD}(2\bar{\zeta}_{c}-\zeta_{c\rm n})>0
, this mode is always stable. if
\bar{\lambda}_{\rm UD}(2\bar{\zeta}_{c}-\zeta_{c\rm n})<0
, the system becomes unstable above a critical length
L>L^{*}
, with
L^{*}
given by

\displaystyle L^{*}=\sqrt{\frac{\kappa K\pi^{2}(4\eta+\gamma)}{2\eta\gamma\bar{\lambda}_{\rm UD}(2\bar{\zeta}_{c}-\zeta_{c\rm n})(\Delta\mu)^{2}}}~.

(386)
